!I 
INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
Until this point I have focused on Harmonic Elliott Wave. Why is this? Very 
simply because it is the only tool that can provide accurate targets, a logical and 
consistent price structure that is controlled by equally consistent ratio clusters. I 
have provided examples where the development has been perfect along with 
others that have not. Over a range of forecasts we will never be 100% accurate. 
With lower degree noise there is risk of incorrectly identifying Wave (i) and 
Wave (ii). Needless to say, to forecast the rest of the sequence in Wave (iii), (iv) 
and (v) relies totally on the first two waves. If these are incorrect then we'll not 
be able to forecast the rest of the waves. 
Again, particularly in the lower wave degrees where there is greater noise and 
volatility, the potential for sharp moves after an economic release or the 
complications of a complex correction, the risk of something going wrong is 
much higher. This is why I maintain that Harmonic Elliott Wave is an indicator 
and not a signal system (as most want it to be . . .  ) 
There are many characteristics to price behavior that can be measured in the 
form of indicators. There are many at our disposal - these can be measurements of 


=== PAGE 125 ===

120 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
price movement when in a consolidating market as well as a trending market, of 
support and resistance and even from tools such as the Commitment of Traders 
Report. Not only can they be applied on a 5-minute chart, but hourly, 4-hour and 
any timeframe you wish to choose. Each provides information about different 
elements of price. Together, this approach can generate more robust information 
and signals. Ignoring them will leave the analyst/trader short of information. 
If we're talking about indicators, would anyone just take Welles Wilder's RSI 
and base all trades on that one indicator? I doubt there are any, and even if they 
do just use RSI, then I'm certain there will be a high degree of errors. In the same 
way, why ignore any other indicator when it is possible to use an array of 
indicators? 
When I was supporting bank traders I would receive requests to program 
trading systems. The number of occasions I had the same request (along with the 
request not to reveal their secret) to code a system was really quite funny. 
Basically, this system would take several momentum indicators such as RSI, 
MACD, Stochastics and CCI and generate a signal when they all are oversold or 
overbought. Needless to say, these systems were definite losers because they all 
essentially measuring the same thing. 
Maybe they filtered them, but the use of momentum in different algorithms 
really only generates a single piece of information: what is momentum 
indicating? This is rather a one-dimensional view of price action. Very clearly we 
want to know more about price. When does a consolidation end and a trend 
begin? How do we measure acceleration in a trend? Where is there support or 
resistance? From where are these support and resistance areas derived? Other 
traders like using volume. What about timing? Can we exploit cycles? Can we 
identify commonly seen stalling areas? 
While I focus on technical indicators exclusively, I would not criticize any 
analyst/trader who utilizes fundamental inputs? I know many who use the 
Commitment of Traders Report to judge overbought and oversold. What are the 
underlying economic indications implying? Personally, I find fundamental 
information tends to lag but it's not a scientific process. 
All in all, every bit of information is valuable and builds a more 
comprehensive picture of a currency, index, stock, commodity or interest rate. It 
just seems crazy to focus on only one element (including economics) to make 
your trading decisions. 
In this chapter, while I cannot cover every single indicator in the market, I will 
provide a general template that can be used to build up information about price. I 
am not an economist and will not include that information. This is something you 
must do for yourself. However, I will describe my favored tools and how I 
integrate them to greater effect, beginning with momentum. 


=== PAGE 126 ===

MOMENTUM 
INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 121 
At its core, momentum is the measurement of both direction and speed of 
price. The formulas used to measure momentum are many but all basically 
provide similar information. Here are just a few: 
Momentum 
Price now - Price X periods ago 
This is basic raw movement between two time points. 
Fig 6-1 .  Simple momentum in the hourly chart of EURUSD 
Momentum merely provides the information of how many points current price 
has travelled from a fixed number of bars before. Normally this will be between 
one close price and a subsequent close price (although technical analysis software 
will normally allow the user to select which price element he or she wishes.) It 
does not provide information about any other price point in between. Clearly, it 
oscillates around a zero line and thus if current price is below that of X bars ago, 
momentum will be negative while if current price is above that of X bars ago, 
momentum will be positive. However, there is no consistency in terms of where 
price is oversold or overbought or the relative speed of price direction. In this 
basic form, momentum provides limited information. 


=== PAGE 127 ===

122 I I NTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
Relative Price Index 
RSI was developed by Welles Wilder Jnr. who, aware of the simplistic nature 
of basic momentum, generated a formula that would measure the relative 
performance of price movement. This basically measured the bar-by-bar net 
movement from close price to close price and separated the positive bar close 
movements from the negative bar close movements. He then ran the results 
through a running average to smooth the sequence and normalized the outcome 
between zero and one hundred. 
Fig 6-2. Welles Wilder's RSI in the hourly chart ofEURUSD 
Fig 6-2 displays the same chart as in Fig 6-1 but with Welles Wilder's RSI 
applied. In general, with most (if not all) momentum indicators there are 
overbought and oversold lines that suggest when price could see a reversal. This 
needs to be applied with care. Overbought and oversold signals are only 
generated when price is not trending. This can be achieved by just eyeballing the 
chart to ensure that price has been moving persistently in one direction. The 
problem I have with RSI is that even when not trending, as seen towards the top 
left of the chart, RSI does not provide a signal for the second two peaks. It is also 
very clear that even in a trend RSI rarely moves above the overbought line in a 
move lower and thus no signal is given. Many analysts then use a lower 
overbought level below the standard input of 70 in a downtrend - or a higher 
oversold level above the standard input of 30. On occasions, I have even seen 
RSI fail to reach either overbought or oversold for the entirety of the data on the 
chart! Of course, there are other indicators that can take over during trends. These 


=== PAGE 128 ===

INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES i 123 
adjustments should be made with care because it implies that the analyst is 
anticipating and therefore has a bias. This may not always be a valid bias . . .  
Another feature of momentum indicators in general is, towards the end of the 
trend when the pace of move is slowing, momentum will display a divergence. 
This can be defined as: 
Bullish divergence: Price lows are declining while momentum lows are rising 
Bearish divergence: Price highs are rising while momentum highs are declining 
Fig 6-3. A bearish divergence in the daily chart ofEURUSD 
In Fig 6-3 the development of a bearish divergence has been seen with price 
highs rising while RSI highs are declining. This basically reflects the fact that the 
pace of the rally is slowing and may generate a reversal lower. However, a 
divergence is not an automatic signal to reverse a position. As can be seen, from 
the first peak to the middle left of the chart, there are three more price peaks. 
Thus, blindly selling once a divergence has been registered can cause significant 
losses. On occasions price can even renew the trend with greater vigor. 
Therefore, technically, the correct way to tackle this is to wait until the most 
recent swing low is broken. There are alternatives, as shown in Fig 6-3, where a 
price reversal pattern is activated - in this case break of the rising wedge support 
will provide the safest signal. Of course, if at the peak there was a significant 
economic trigger then the potential to follow the divergence is a valid option. 


=== PAGE 129 ===

124 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
However, in this case, there was another signal (even other than Harmonic 
Elliott Wave) that suggested losses. Both weekly and daily charts provided a Key 
Reversal Day and Key Reversal Week that confirmed the risk of losses. 
Of course, the same approach of using bullish divergences and break above 
prior swing highs is also implied following a downtrend. 
At this point I will introduce my own RSI. Having found the original rather 
difficult to work with I decided to develop my own. At first sight it may look 
slightly better but, when I come to discuss multiple time-frame charts, it will be 
obvious how it provides stronger indications. 
Fig 6-4. HEW RSI applied to the daily chart ofEURUSD 
The chart above in Fig 6-4 is exactly the same chart but with my own RSI. Not 
only does it provide divergences, but also sees the swings in momentum reach the 
overbought and oversold areas more frequently unlike the original. Compare the 
overbought and oversold indications and also, in some cases, intermediate bearish 
and bullish divergences. In general, I find the "double divergence," as seen in the 
initial October through end March rally, followed by the higher price peaks in 
April when HEW RSI generated a lower peak provided a stronger signal. This 
"double divergence" tends to have a high instance of a larger reversal. 
Of course, this is my preference and what I like to see, but other analysts will 
prefer their own indicators. 
Later I shall demonstrate other uses of HEW RSI. 


=== PAGE 130 ===

INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 1 125 
MACD - Moving Average Divergence and Convergence 
MACD is another popular momentum indicator. Instead of measuring the rate 
of change in price, it takes two exponential moving averages and measures the 
difference between one average and the other. Thus, as price direction slows, the 
difference between the averages will narrow; while as price direction accelerates 
the difference between the averages will widen. Consequently, if price is trending 
higher the shorter average will be above the longer average and vice versa. 
Fig 6-5. MACD applied to the hourly chart in EURUSD 
In Fig 6-5 it can be seen that MACD oscillates around a zero line and during 
trends there are longer periods when MACD remains above zero in an uptrend 
and below zero in a downtrend. As a basic explanation, when the MACD crosses 
above the signal line it may suggest a reversal higher, and vice versa. However, 
it's not quite that simple, since a rising MACD below zero is more often a sign of 
a decelerating downtrend while a falling MACD above zero can be a deceleration 
in an uptrend. Many traders like to use the MACD cross as a signal but even just 
eyeballing the chart it can be seen that the signal needs to be filtered. The 
histogram around the zero line merely indicates that the shorter exponential 
moving average has crossed above the longer exponential moving average and 
vtce versa. 
MACD provides bullish and bearish divergences although, as seen in the above 
chart, do not always confirm a reversal. Clearly, some other technique (or 
techniques) must be added to confirm a reversal. 


=== PAGE 131 ===

126 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
It is not my favorite indicator but is a very popular one in the market but has a 
tendency to join trends late.) 
STOCHASTICS 
The Stochastic indicator was developed by George Lane and is basically a 
percentage measurement of where current price is now in relation to the range 
(Highest high to lowest low over a set period of bars.) 
%K = (Current Close - Lowest Low)/(Highest High - Lowest Low) * 1 00 
%D = 3-day SMA of%K 
Lowest Low = lowest low for the look -back period 
Highest High = highest high for the look -back period 
%K is multiplied by 1 00 to make the scale between zero and 1 00 
The formula generates the basic stochastic and from there it can be smoothed 
again to slow the oscillation of the indicator.

From Fig 6-6 it can be seen that Stochastics, this being the slower signal, 
generates far stronger swings between overbought and oversold - together with 
periods of increasingly volatile swings within a tighter range (in Stochastics) 
during tight consolidation or when price continues in one direction. There is even 
one point (that was also seen in RSI and to a certain extent in MACD) where a 
bullish divergence developed. What can we deduce from these movements? 
There is no definitive answer to this. Many use Stochastics when they are 
either rising or falling as a confirmation of a directional move to follow the trend. 
However, we have no idea where price will stall. Another use is that when 
Stochastics has moved to one extreme in a trend with no divergence that it is 
unlikely to be the final extreme and thus trade into the reversal when Stochastics 
reaches the opposite extreme. Still, as can be seen that after the low (following 
the bullish divergence) Stochastics rallies to overbought, dips a bit, makes a 
second attempt back above overbought, before dropping. Regardless, there's no 
consistent correlation with price overall. 
I will stop at this point when considering Stochastics and return to these 
concepts following the last momentum study I listed above to highlight. an 
additional feature that can strengthen the signals. 
CCI - Commodity Channel Index 
CCI was developed by Donald Lambert to identify extreme conditions and 
identify reversals. It basically measures current price levels relative to an average 
Fig 6-7. CCI applied to the hourly chart in EURUSD 


=== PAGE 133 ===

128 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
price over a period of time and, like the overbought and oversold lines in RSI and 
Stochastics, he used a level of (plus) 70-80 as overbought and (minus) 70-80 as 
oversold. 
As can be seen, CCI produces a significantly volatile series but focuses on the 
periods above or below the extreme settings. Like other momentum indicators, it 
also generates bullish and bearish divergences that, as described before, require 
confirmation and are not an automated signal. Perhaps its most significant use can 
be the limited pullback following reaching an extreme and then seeing the CCI 
reading push back to the same extreme as a sign of a trend. Eventually a 
divergence will then develop and suggest the potential for a reversal of the trend. 
SUMMARY 
What information are we expecting to receive using momentum indicators? My 
own personal opinion is the following: 
Momentum can suggest: 
RSI 
• 
Price direction 
• 
Relative acceleration or deceleration of movement 
• 
Potential recognition of a trend 
• 
Indications of a potential reversal in price

When considered from another perspective, it also means that momentum 
cannot tell us where a price movement will stall, nor whether we'll see a 
correction or a total reversal. This reaffirms the bullet list above - that all we can 
expect is to garner information about the current direction of price and its relative

indicators on the charts is not enough to be able to judge whether any reversal 
will be to generate a new trend or just a correction.

The result is very different. I have highlighted the key swing highs and swing

Clearly, Welles Wilder's RSI provided the fewest reversal indications, whilst 
Stochastics provided four reversal indications, MACD one, CCI six and my RSI 
five (plus the indication second from the left which suggested that, if there was a 
reversal, it would likely cause a new high.)

Strangely enough, at the second peak from the left none of the indicators 
provided a signal. Even then, the next problem that we need to solve is where 
price will stall and reverse. This is something only Harmonic Elliott Wave can 
cope with. It was due to what I felt were shortcomings in Welles Wilder's RSI, 
MACD and Stochastics that I began to look at producing my own RSI. 
SUPPORT AND RESISTANCE 
In chapter four I discussed the natural support and resistance areas that are 
generated from both Harmonic Elliott Wave guidelines, along with those that are 
derived from previous support or resistance. We could also include trend lines 
although I tend to find there are few valid trend lines that can be drawn. However, 
I will dedicate a section of this topic later on as I find that trend lines are both 
misunderstood and abused. 
I shall not cover the natural support and resistance here. Instead I'll address 
support and resistance in trends and highlight potential initial reversal points, 
through indicators and valid trend lines. 
TREND LINES 
While not strictly an indicator, I will include these because their similar role. 
Fig 6-1 0. Non-trend lines drawn on the daily chart ofEURUSD 


=== PAGE 136 ===

INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 131 
As I have mentioned, I believe trend lines have been abused terribly and are not 
fully understood. First let me provide a chart of what I normally see from many 
chartists. 
In Fig 6-10 I have drawn one line across some peaks and three lines below 
troughs. It is all too common to see lines drawn across peaks and the statement 
that the market is going to drop. Well, at the first new high after the peak labeled 
1 we could only judge that it would be a peak once the reversal had been seen. 
Therefore there is no indication from a trend line to suggest a reversal. Therefore 
the "trend line" is only valid after that second touch. 
From that high price drops below a supporting "trend line" (labeled 2) and this 
tends to get analysts excited. However, instead of extending losses in a true 
bearish move, it merely reverses higher from just a bit lower and then reverses 
back higher and breaks back above the No. 2 trend line to a new peak. Price 
breaks above the No. 1 trend line and reverses. Once again, the analyst goes 
through the process of drawing trend line No. 3 which, on the reversal, breaks and 
the analyst gets excited again - but only to see another reversal higher to another 
new peak, followed by a correction so that trend line No. 4 can be drawn that 
actually did support the correction and move to a marginal new high before the 
total reversal. 
So what has gone wrong? The clue is in the word "trend." So, what is the 
definition of a trend? This is all too often forgotten by analysts: 
Uptrend: 
Downtrend: 
A sequence of higher highs and higher lows 
A sequence of lower lows and lower highs 
If we try and apply that definition to the trend lines in Fig 6- 1 0  we can see that 
there are no trends where the lines are drawn. I have had analysts arguing that a 
trend line can be drawn below two consecutive lows or above two consecutive 
highs. Well, that can be achieved even in a correction. Clearly, from the example 
above it is not enough. 
I also consider that if price deviates from the trend line by too far, then it can 
no longer be considered a trend. To me, it is important that the trend is a sequence 
that is direct and not part of a larger swing move. 
I have drawn some trend lines on the weekly chart of AUDUSD in Fig 6-1 1 .  
The two (shorter) steeper trend supports look more like rallies that are seeing 
higher highs and higher lows. The second looks reasonable also. However, the 
longer line that is drawn below the deeper correction in the middle of the chart is 
certainly not a trend because price had deviated from the line by too much. Had 
the correction from the final peak at the top right remained above the line, then I 
would have considered a trend. 


=== PAGE 137 ===

132 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
Weekry 
Fig 6-1 1  displaying lines drawn in the daily chart in AUDUSD 
Trend lines can be considered but rarely do they work precisely but when they 
do we should also expect the lower timeframe charts to see divergences in 
momentum indicators to confirm a reversal. Attempting to buy at a line as seen in 
the (third) longer trend support drawn with no supporting evidence would be 
highly risky. 
Indeed, when we have a tool like Harmonic Elliott Wave, it is possible to make 
better judgments as to the basic structure and the likely stalling points by using a 
forecasting tool rather than rely on a line that may or may not work. 
In general, I rarely draw trend lines and basically never rely on them. However, 
if we're talking about lines used to define a pattern, such as a wedge, then I will 
happily use it as long as it confirms the indication I get from Harmonic Elliott 
Wave. 
Such an event happened a few weeks ago when I forecast a Wave iii high in 
the Dow Jones Industrial Index. It had to correct by 58.6% and in a probable 
complex correction, due to the fact that Wave ii was so brief and shallow. The 
58.6% retracement was expected at 1 6,262 but actually stalled just above at 
1 6,333.78. 
This is shown in Fig 6-1 2  in which we saw a sharp decline, as would be 
expected after the wedge support had broken and reached the low of the Wave b 
of Wave iii. Note also how HEW RSI also provided a solid bearish divergence to 
confirm the wedge and break of the wedge low. In the end, the Wave iv wasn't as 
accurate as expected but, as discussed in the example of the sharp reversal from 
1 .3993 in EURUSD, these early waves that are not clearly defined do 
occasionally see the Wave iv fall short.

Very clearly from these examples, it can be seen that trend lines are abused to a

important to ensure that if a bounce from that line is expected then the usual 
requirements for a reversal - in terms of reversal patterns and momentum 
indicators - should be adhered to.

As an example of something I see quite regularly is what I call "non trend 
lines." I have to admit that there are instances where these can work, but in 
general many traders/analysts just keep drawing lines that come to nothing. Most 
of the time it is because there is not enough thought put into identifying a trend 
by its definition and merely drawing a line. 
Here, we are beginning to see how integrating several techniques at the same 
time helps to provide much stronger signals. 
BOLLINGER BANDS 
One source of potential support and resistance can be from Bollinger Bands, 
developed by John Bollinger. These are very basic standard deviation bands 
around a central (20 period) moving average. The indicators are defaulted to + 2 
and -2 standard deviations from the average, a level that statistically encompasses 
around 95% of all data. Thus, while price remains in a consolidation we would 
expect Bollinger Bands to provide support and resistance. During trending 
conditions price will penetrate one of the bands and continue in that direction. 
The central moving average is then considered an approximate stop loss. 
ጚ1.35000

Fig 6-13. Combined 4-hour and hourly charts on EURUSD with Bollinger

Since I am insistent that indicators must be viewed in multiple time frames I 
have, in Fig 6-1 3, applied Bollinger Bands to both the 4-hour chart to the right 
and hourly to the left in EURUSD. At Point 1 we can see that price consolidates 
and the upside corrections do penetrate the hourly Bollinger Bands while the 4-

hour Bollinger Bands, based on a larger timeframe, provide general resistance. 
Clearly, we would have to be prepared for a rally should those peaks break. 
However, price continues to decline and, until the base just before the 
correction at Point 2, the central moving average provided resistance in the 
downtrend. The correction at Point 2 saw the central moving average in the 4-
hour chart provide resistance to allow the downtrend to resume. 
As the downtrend continues another correction develops to rally to Point 3. 
While price penetrates the hourly Bollinger Band high it stalls at the 4-hour 
Bollinger Band high. 
In this manner we can begin to first judge where price can stall and also match 
this with the Harmonic Elliott Wave count.

Now, as we integrate the momentum indicators and Bollinger Bands with the 
Harmonic Elliott Wave count, we can begin to see how the combination of 
multiple timeframe indicators complement and match the wave count. The same 
points are highlighted to show where the 4-hour Bollinger Bands provided 
resistance. This would have helped at Point 2 where we have an unknown 
corrective ratio. By the time we get to the projection in Wave [iii] (that saw a 
bullish divergence - although 4-hour HEW RSI was reaching overbought) we can 
match the expected Wave [iv] retracement with the Bollinger Band high. 
At this point price is moving lower in what should be Wave [ c] of Wave [ v]. 
However, the bullish divergence has broken and which raises a level of caution in 
terms of whether the wave count is correct. Much will depend on how the 
remainder of the decline in Wave [ c] develops. We shall probably need to see a

deep Wave b of the lower degree Wave iii to ensure that momentum generates a 
bullish divergence. 
PRICE EQUILffiRIUM CLOUDS 
When I first arrived in Japan, one of the first indicators I noted was Ichimoku 
Kinko Hyo, roughly translated by my colleagues as "Once Glance Cloud Cover." 
It requires, I was informed, the need to read at least nine books including one on 
Buddhism in order to understand how it was used. Alas, my Japanese skills never 
rose to that standard! Therefore, I never really took time to investigate although I 
have seen other non-Japanese use the technique and witnessed some good 
analysis from Japanese analysts. 
In the end I decided I'd develop my own, built around my concepts of how I 
saw price development and which could complement my momentum indicators 
along with Harmonic Elliott Wave. Once again, I apply these over multiple time 
frame charts as a stronger integrated framework of analysis. The Price 
Equilibrium Cloud is essentially an indicator that provides information about the 
extent of the trend and where price equilibrium rests within that trend. It is in 
some ways similar to a moving average but it contains an algorithm that filters the 
information to judge the relevance of the movement, which acts like a variable 
input.

In Fig 6-1 5  I have used the same sequence in the downtrend in EURUSD but

240-minute and daily. The Price Equilibrium Clouds are designed to hug price

closely in a trend and for any corrections to stall within the next higher timeframe 
Cloud or with only a minor breach. It can be seen that they have a similar impact 
to the Bollinger Bands but the key trigger for losses at Point 1 was the steeply 
declining daily Cloud. Of course, we would be using Harmonic Elliott Wave as 
the primary forecasting tool, with the indicators merely as complementary aid. 
However, in addition to the impact in a trend, HEW Price Equilibrium Clouds 
also provide information at turning points. 
Fig 6-16. Multiple timeframe Price Equilibrium Clouds in the S&P 500 Index 
Fig 6-16 demonstrates the characteristics of the Price Equilibrium Clouds 
during a reversal in the S&P 500 Index across the 60-minute, 240-minute and 
daily charts. As price begins to become more volatile in the intraday charts, it can 
be seen that price begins to swing around the Clouds but holding above the daily 
Cloud. By the time price reaches the lower degree Wave iii and Wave iv (not 
labeled) the 240-minute and daily HEW RSI provide bearish divergences. 
Normally, the Clouds begin to flatten out before they are penetrated which, in this 
daily reversal, can be seen quite clearly. 
In this reversal, note how the 240-minute chart sees another break below the 
240-minute Price Equilibrium Cloud but on this occasion, instead of pushing 
back above the Cloud, it merely retests and then resumes losses below the daily 
Cloud. 
The decline from this Wave [iii] high (see Fig 6-1 7) is much more robust and 
as the hourly chart develops into a Wave [iv] and later Wave [v]. In the decline to 
Wave [iv], it can be seen that the 60-minute Cloud provides resistance for the 
corrections (including the Wave iv) allowing a 60-minute bullish divergence to 
develop - which are also noted to a lesser extent in the 240-minute and daily 
charts. With the bullish divergence and break back above the hourly Cloud, price 
rallies and stalls at the declining 240-minute Cloud (circled in Fig 6- 16) and 


=== PAGE 143 ===

138 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
corrects lower within the Cloud. This is quite common. Then finally, when price 
breaks back above the 240-minute Cloud it can be seen that the daily Cloud has 
flattened out to allow price to resume the rally. 
Fig 6-1 7. Momentum and Cloud positions after the break higher in Wave [v] 
In Fig 6-1 7  I have added the Harmonic Elliott Wave count to the hourly and 
daily charts. Note that the peak before the larger decline was the Wave [iii] and 
that the Wave [ii] (not displayed) was both extremely brief and shallow, requiring 
a deeper Wave [iv] retracement - and a high risk of a complex correction. 
However, like the example of the Dow Jones Industrial Index in Fig 6-1 2, the low 
at 1 ,904.78 actually completed the entire correction in Wave [iv]. 
Following on from Wave -iii- high, the break below the hourly & 4-hour Price 
Equilibrium Clouds in Fig 6-16, the daily Cloud had begun to flatten out 
following the modest correction in Wave iv (see Fig 6-1 7) and this allowed losses 
to develop in Wave -iv-. It is quite common for the price structure to become a 
little more complicated as a price reversal is expected, which raises the likelihood 
of a reversal lower. 
In summary, the combination of a group of indicators that provide different and 
complementary information about what is happening, and which provides the 
right signals to confirm the Harmonic Elliott Wave count will provide more 
robust and safer analysis. 
COUNTER INDICATIONS 
However, what happens if indicators are suggestive of a different outcome 
when compared to the Harmonic Elliott Wave count? The answer is: firstly, 


=== PAGE 144 ===

INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES i 139 
approach with caution and secondly, review of the wave count. Certainly, it 
would be prudent to at least reduce position size and possibly just exit the trade 
until a stronger signal is given. Indicators can contribute to this.

There will always be occasions where the structure is a little mixed. I had 
originally been looking for a high to develop in Wave (a) a little above where it 
has been labeled and for a correction lower in Wave (b). Indeed, a bearish 
divergence had developed in the 60-minute chart and this began to look as if I 
was correct in my expectations. However, as price continued to push higher, with 
both 4-hour and hourly Price Equilibrium Clouds supportive, the bearish 
divergence broke. This turned the outlook directly bullish through a combination 
of the supporting Clouds along with the rising hourly and 4-hour HEW RSI. I 
invariably find that a combination of these two momentum indicators rising 
simultaneously will always generate strength, and of course the opposite if they 
are both falling when above/below the overbought/oversold lines respectively. 
This is an example of when, even just the combination of multi timeframe 
indicators confirming the same outcome, the more aggressive directional 
momentum will override the Harmonic Elliott Wave count. In this example, the 
fact that the Price Equilibrium Clouds pointed to the same outcome, 1s an 
automatic trading signal. 
So it can be seen that multi-layered and multi-timeframe approach using 
Harmonic Elliott Wave with complementary indicators provides far greater depth 
to the analysis and both provides great signals and also, at times, warnings that 
something else is going to happen. 


=== PAGE 145 ===

140 j iNTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES 
TIME CYCLES 
As a final discussion on indicators I will demonstrate a tool that can help with 
market timing and structure. However, this is a very deep subject and one that 
cannot be covered in full within this book but will hopefully provide an indication 
of the additional information they provide. I should also add that these are long­
term cycles which do not yield the same results within shorter time frames, so I 
mostly use weekly and monthly charts. 
I have to add that the cycles I use are fixed and are restricted to a limited 
number of Foreign Exchange pairs. I shall therefore focus on the set of major 
currency pairs that display the ability to remain consistent: The Dollar Index, 
USDJPY, GBPUSD and AUDUSD. The history for EURUSD is far too short and 
although I do see EURUSD with longer history before July 1 st 1 999 it is derived 
from trade movement rather than real historical trading. In addition, prior to the 
Euro being introduced, the European currencies were quoted directly against the 
Dollar (amount of currency units to the Dollar). Following the introduction of the 
Euro however, it became an indirect currency pair (that is it is quoted as an 
amount of Dollars to one Euro.) In this respect, because cycles are measured from 
low to low, the change from being quoted directly to the Dollar to indirectly has 
turned the chart upside down. 
I do have quite a few clients who use Delta Cycles. I have been meaning to 
study these but have never had enough time to fully immerse myself into this 
subject because Harmonic Elliott Wave keeps me so busy. From the limited 
information I have gleaned, it is relatively straightforward but does have its 
moments that lack clarity with inverted cycles and this is where my subscribers 
like to match expectations through the Harmonic Elliott Wave outlook. I do 
recommend the Delta Cycles but unfortunately cannot provide a sufficiently 
informed description of them. 
The basic concept behind fixed cycles is that natural events occur in 
recognizable cycles, e.g. the period taken by planets to circle the sun, the Earth's 
revolution around the Sun which produces the four seasons, the revolution of the 
moon around the Earth which affects the tidal patterns of the oceans, etc. Intense 
research has been made into cycles by Edward R. Dewy, Stan Erlich and Walter 
Bressert, who have noted such cycles as the 9.6-year cycle in salmon abundance, 
a 22-year cycle in international battles, an 1 1 -year cycle in sunspot activity and 
even the 60-year cycle of economic depression. We readily accept many of these 
cycles in our daily lives: re-occurring business cycles, industrial expansion and 
recession. It should come as no surprise that these are seen in market behavior 
also. 


=== PAGE 146 ===

INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES ! 141 
Cyclic Principals 
There are four basic principles associated with cycles and a basic 
understanding of these would assist in applying cycle analysis. 
-
Summation 
- Harmonicity 
-
Synchronicity 
- Proportionality

"added" together

ů----
Cycle 
Fig 6-19. The impact of summation, harmonicity, synchronicity and 
proportionality 
From Fig 6-19 it can be seen that in fixed cycles the amplitude of the next 
higher cycle will be two (proportionality), the cycle troughs will develop at the 
same time (synchronicity) and the power of each successively larger cycle will 
increase by two (harmonicity.) Although not easily shown in this image, the 
strength of each next higher cycle will double (summation.) If these cycles were 
displayed as sine waves, moving above and below a zero line it would be possible 
to measure the positive and negative impact of each cycle. 


=== PAGE 147 ===

142 I INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES

increasing bullish pressure and vice versa. To demonstrate how this works, I have 
overlaid the cycles on the monthly chart ofGBPUSD in Fig 6-20.

Fig 6-20. Cycles applied to the monthly chart of GBPUSD 
I provided this example of the monthly cycles in GBPUSD in my book 
"Integrated Technical Analysis" in 1999. The cycles have continued to work 
exceptionally well ever since and currently GBPUSD is beginning a significant 
decline. Whether this will reach back to the 1 .0370 low or not by mid 201 7, when 
the confluence of the four cycles occurs, is unclear. If it does, then we may be 
talking about a flat correction. If it fails above 1 .0370, we may just see a Wave 
(x). However, during 201 5  we should begin to see more robust losses. 
Translation 
What is noticeable is that while price tends to see major cycle lows where there 
are two cycles (or more) the price peaks are not necessarily equidistant as 
between the cycle highs. This is displayed in Fig 6-21 and shows the effect 
known as "translation" that identifies whether the peaks are moving higher or 
lower. In general, since the 1 .03 70 low in 1 985 and observing the second largest 
cycle, it can be seen that the final price peaks before the descent into the cycle 
lows are very close to the end of the cycle and after the cycle high has been seen. 
This is "right translation" that suggests the larger cycles are still pushing higher 
(the effect of summation) and dragging price with it. This implies a more bullish 


=== PAGE 148 ===

INTEGRATING INDICATORS AND CYCLES ! 143 
Right Translatidn (Bullish) 
Fig 6-2 1 .  The impact oftranslation 
outcome once the major cycle low is seen. This may well answer the query I 
made on whether the losses into 2017 will reach 1 .0370 or not. That is something 
that will have to be observed as the cycle low occurs . . .  
Where there is less clarity is in identifying the cycle peaks. This will need to be 
judged through Harmonic Elliott Wave along with momentum. At this point, 
rather than providing examples in this section, I'd prefer to continue in the next 
chapter by bringing together the various groups of techniques I have described 
until this point and demonstrate how these can be fused to greater effect. 
EXAMPLES OF CYCLES & INDICATOR COMBINATIONS 
GBPUSD 
I shall provide two examples to demonstrate how I would approach the 
confirmation of a cycle low, the first being in GBPUSD. Just above, in Fig 6.20, 
the monthly cycles displayed some exceptionally accurate cycles throughout from 
the early 1970's. It would be useful just to flip back a page or two to observe 
these cycles and, in particular, the low in January 2009 at 1 .3501 . The monthly 
HEW RSI provided a relatively weak bullish divergence about six months before 
the precise cycle low. However, this is not such a wide margin when considering 
the second largest cycle is 196 months.

Clearly, during the decline the objective would have been to identify a 5-wave 
decline. As this wave moved into a Wave (iv) and Wave (v) the emphasis will be 
on identifying the end of Wave (c) of Wave (v). This can be seen in Fig 6-22.

Fig 6-22. The monthly and daily charts in GBPUSD for the cycle low 
The monthly chart is to the left. Note the bullish divergence developing as the 
cycle low approaches. The Price Equilibrium Cloud, once price broke below the 
consolidation following Wave (i) and (ii), followed price in its sharp decline. 
Once the Wave (iii) was reached the decline obviously began to slow to provoke 
a greater number of swings, initially in Wave (iv) and then the Wave (b) of Wave 
(v). Following the low the Cloud began to flatten out which is a normal reaction 
in most instances. This capped price in a consolidation following the low. 
In a monthly chart, the granularity is not really conducive to establishing 
whether this was the actual low. However, the mere fact that we had a bullish 
divergence is a warning sign, as is the flattening Price Equilibrium Cloud. 
The chart to the right is the daily chart. The bullish divergence is obviously a 
very strong one, running from the Wave iii of Wave (c) I (iii) to the final low at 
Wave (v) I (A). While price does begin to oscillate around the daily Price 
Equilibrium Cloud, it is not really a problem because the monthly Cloud is 
providing a barrier. 
At what stage would a long position be established? If confident, and 
confirmed by the 4-hour and hourly charts, a position could be established quite 
early. However, knowing that a sharper recovery is unlikely it would be better to 
reduce, or eliminate, the position as the monthly Cloud remains declining to flat. 
This process could be repeated as the base Wave i and Wave ii legs are 
established but then look for a break above the weekly and monthly Price 
Equilibrium Clouds.

INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH INDICATORS 
Until this point I have tended to focus on individual facets of analysis although 
have attempted to provide some integration in some examples. The core price 
indicator is Harmonic Elliott Wave. It has been the best forecasting tool I have 
ever used but there can be problems: 
- Naturally not all forecasts develop exactly as expected 
- Misjudgment, particularly in a "noisy" market where identifying the Wave 
i and Wave ii can produce problems and errors 
- Alternatively, some development can have too little noise that makes the 
identification of waves more difficult 
- There are waves where there can be a range of projection or corrective 
ratios 
-
Daily misjudgments can increase errors in the lower timeframe waves 


=== PAGE 155 ===

150 ! INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTI WAVE WITH INDICATORS 
Therefore we should, and must, observe other factors about price that may help 
provide stronger information about price. In this chapter I shall be providing a 
range of examples of how to integrate a group of techniques to strengthen the 
analysis, to confirm trends and perhaps the opposite, that we'll see a trend 
reversal or consolidation. 
In general, where I can, I like to have a sense of: 
-
The price outlook through Harmonic Elliott Wave 
o The status across the wave degrees 
o Trend versus corrective outlooks 
o Potential projection ratios 
o The areas where the structure will break down 
-
The current status of momentum 
o Comparison across timeframes 
o Status if approaching support or resistance targets 
- Potential support or resistance levels 
o Natural Elliott based support or resistance 
o Prior support or resistance on retest 
o Price Equilibrium Clouds 
o Multiple timeframe indications 
-
If available key cycle timings 
o These tend to be appropriate for a limited number of Forex currency 
pairs and only over longer time periods 
o How the wave structure is expected to develop and suggest when 
trends may end and corrections begin 
-
Cross market analysis 
o Potential correlation between related market vehicles 
STRUCTURING THE PRICE ANALYSIS 
I normally see many Elliott Wave analysts focusing on one timeframe and 
reacting to current events. To be fair, they tend to take this approach because 
there isn't any way to really link the wave degrees in traditional Elliott Wave. 
This is really the wrong approach. Before being able to consider the lower 
degree development it is critical to know what the higher degree wave 
development implies. When tackling a new market I will always seek to research 
the monthly or weekly charts. Where possible, if I can establish a strong wave 
structure in, say, the weekly chart I will then go down to the daily chart and check 
the lower degree structure to ensure that the Wave (a) and Wave (c) moves are 


=== PAGE 156 ===

INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH INDICATORS 151 
indeed constructed of 5-waves and with a valid ratio structure. Even then, it must 
be understood that a daily bar can hide key lower degree waves that could negate 
the analysis. However, the basic fact that there appears to be a valid structure will 
demand that a particular move should occur - whether 5-wave or 3-wave. 
This entire process should, wherever possible, be repeated in the lower degree 
charts - the hourly at a bare minimum over recent development. Throughout the 
different levels of wave degrees checks should be made for alternation as a means 
of confirming the structure has validity. In this way, it is possible to have more 
confidence in what to expect - affirmative in terms of progression of the structure 
or where it will break down. 
Let me provide the example ofUSDJPY.

MOMENTUM ANALYSIS 
This is quite a simple process but when used carefully it can generate a lot of 
information that can help understand whether price will stall and reverse (perhaps 
completely or just for a correction) or whether the trend will extend. Many 
analysts will use Welles Wilder's ADX (Average Directional Movement) that 
attempts to identify when a trend has begun to develop. Definitely, if a trend is in 
place ADX will continue to rise. However, it is rather a slow indicator in terms of 
confirming a trend and also when the trend stalls. Moving averages are lagging by 
nature and slow to confirm a reversal, tending to give back much of the profit -
assuming the crossovers are used strictly. 
However, we have Harmonic Elliott Wave as a price indicator that provides 
high-risk areas for Wave (iii) projection targets and because of the wave structure 
we'll have an idea of whether a Wave (iv) will be shallow or deep, or even 
whether this is just a Wave (iii) within a higher Wave (III) that would imply 
further gains. Thus, moving averages tend to become redundant through lack of 
clarity in identifying when a trend is due to end and how deep a correction will 
be. 
t.l,SOO.OO I 
: •• 00000 .I 
j 12,500.00 ' 
(b) -ll· 
Fig 7-5. Use of indicators across time frames in the Wave (c) I (iii) in the DOW 
As an example of just utilizing two timeframes, the weekly and daily charts of 
the rally in the Dow Jones Industrial Index over the Wave (c) of Wave (iii) shown 
in Fig 7-5, I have placed the 50 and 200 period moving averages in the daily chart 
that seems to be favorites for many in the market. Personally I find the signals 
from moving averages far too slow and dismissed moving averages very early in 
my career as an analyst. In some ways the 50 period moving average turns 
relatively soon after the key peaks and troughs within periods of directional 
moves as shown in the example, but there are just as many, or more, that see 


=== PAGE 161 ===

156 I INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH INDICATORS 
more shallow swings that would causes losses. In terms of the cross of the 50 
period average through the 200 period average it leaves virtually no profit. 
This is very inefficient. While we cannot be confident of all turning points with 
Harmonic Elliott Wave we can achieve greater control by combining multiple 
timeframe indicators. 
At turning points there is normally a bullish or bearish divergence in the lower 
timeframe chart, in this case the daily chart and while not drawn, it can be seen 
that we had HEW RSI troughs rising at the Wave (b) low (while the weekly HEW 
RSI was oversold.) Being a Wave (b) it would be prudent to remain cautious due 
to the unknown depth of Wave (b)'s and this could delay an entry should there be 
no price pattern. While it cannot be seen in Fig 7-5 there was a two-bar reversal 
that would have given more confidence. Aggressively it would be possible to take 
a small long position at this stage but would require comfortable stop loss. 
Of course, we have expectations. The Wave (b) developed in a double zigzag 
and therefore the first reversal should move to the span of the prior Wave -b­
(which it did) and then slightly higher towards the prior swing high where it was 
capped by the weekly Price Equilibrium Cloud. At this point the task will be to 
identify the Wave -i- and Wave -ii- to provide the platform for the more 
directional rally in Wave -iii-. 
Fig 7-6. The Wave (b) low in the 4-hour chart 
By looking back to the combination of the weekly and daily charts above we 
can begin to see how the indicators begin to provide information. By adding in

that the Wave -a- was also

Price Equilibrium Cloud. There was a correction in Wave -b-. This would have 
been more difficult to judge although it notably remained above the daily Price 
Equilibrium Cloud low to extend gains to Wave -i- where it reversed at the top of 
the (still declining) weekly Price Equilibrium Cloud. Note there was a bearish 
divergence at Wave -i- and already corrections were testing the 4-hour Price 
Equilibrium Cloud low. Thus, a reversal below the Cloud, on a bearish 
divergence, capped by the weekly Price Equilibrium Cloud and then broke below 
the daily Price Equilibrium Cloud. This multiple and coordinated pattern of 
support/resistance from the multiple timeframe Clouds is a very common 
development on the first reversal after a key high or low. 
The Wave -ii- then developed, capped by the 4-hour Price Equilibrium Cloud, 
developed a bullish divergence for a reversal back higher. Note that just before 
the Wave -ii- there was a low (that would have been a minor Wave iii) followed 
by a correction in Wave iv that was rebuffed by the declining Price Equilibrium 
Cloud. The reversal higher, back above the 4-hour Price Equilibrium Cloud and 
followed by the daily Cloud confirmed the larger rally in Wave -a- of Wave -iii­
that also broke above the Weekly Cloud. From here the risk would have been for 
more steady gains. 
From this point onwards we had a persistent uptrend. This permits the rest of 
the move to develop with the daily Cloud supporting, but when breached, then 
being supported by the weekly Cloud. Of course, during this process we would be 
using the spreadsheet to identify potential stalling points for the Wave iii's and 
corrections in Wave iv and matching these with the daily and/or weekly Clouds 
supporting. 
In terms of following the trend with moving averages the pullback in Wave -iv­
in particular could have taken a trader out of the position with price having 
broken below the 50 period moving average. Indeed, even using Harmonic Elliott 
Wave that could be considered with a re-entry just towards the higher end of the 
expected Wave -iv- retracement range, the weekly Cloud providing confirmation. 
By the time price reached the 12,876 high - the 198.7% projection in Wave 
(iii) - where a daily bearish divergence had developed along with a minor weekly 
divergence there was a strong case to either reduce the position size or even take 
profit well before the 50 period moving average turned lower. . .  


=== PAGE 163 ===

158 j iNTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH INDICATORS 
Just as I have shown the process of the reversal from the Wave (b) low through 
the wave degrees, Fig 7-7 displays the reversal at the Wave (iii) high. The same 
process develops once again. During the tail end of the rally the daily Price 
Equilibrium Cloud broke during the correction in Wave -b- of Wave -v- but was 
supported by the rising weekly Cloud. Rather disappointingly, there was no 4-
hour bearish divergence - an occasional development. However, what 1s 
noticeable was that following the break of the 4-hour Price Equilibrium Cloud 
Fig 7-7. The reversal from the Wave (iii) high in the DOW 
to form a low at Wave -i-, it can be seen in Fig 7-5 that the rising daily Cloud was 
providing support. The recovery in Wave -ii- stalled at the top of the 4-hour 
Cloud just after it began to tum lower. The next decline broke below the daily 
Cloud. Also note that the break back above the 4-hour Cloud to form Wave -b- of 
Wave -iii- was capped by the daily Cloud that had just begun to tum lower. It can 
also been seen that the Wave -a- of Wave -iii- stalled in the span of the prior 
Wave -b- of Wave -v-. 
Once again, this is a common development at market turns that, when used 
along with the Harmonic Elliott Wave structure with its natural targets, the use of 
the spreadsheet to identify key targets or retracements and the Price Equilibrium 
Clouds, the ability to add or lighten position sizes at appropriate times provides a 
much tighter control over positions.

example. 
USDJPY was rallying and I was expecting quite a solid rally. However, this 
was just the start of the move and therefore in the early stages I was preparing for 
a Wave -i- and Wave -ii- to be followed by a Wave -a-.

What eventually developed was extremely difficult to judge accurately. In Fig

through to the Wave -a- and onwards. The Wave -i- and Wave -ii- was 
straightforward. When it came to the Wave -a-, while I have provided the final 
count, as this progressed I was anticipating a higher Wave -a- than actually 
occurred. In fact, I took the high (marked Wave -a-) as the Wave iii being 2 
points above the 198.7% projection. The retracement to where I have labeled 
Wave -b- was a perfect Wave iv retracement and therefore I was looking for a 
peak in Wave v to form Wave -a-. There was an hourly bearish divergence but 
not in the 4-hour chart. However, because the 4-hour chart had not really 
registered a trend this would not have been a problem. 
Then, as price rallied to the target I had set the bearish divergence broke while 
both hourly and 4-hour HEW RSI both began to rally above the overbought line. 
As mentioned earlier, this is a clear sign of strength. In addition, the hourly Price 
Equilibrium Cloud was rising and supporting price, along with the 4-hour Price 


=== PAGE 165 ===

160 I I NTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH INDICATORS 
Equilibrium Cloud backing it up. We therefore had two bullish indications from 
HEW RSI and HEW Price Equilibrium Clouds on both charts. 
While this was enough to follow price higher and forget the expectation of a 
correction in Wave -b-, there was actually another piece of supporting evidence. 
In Fig 7-8 the rally began from Wave [ii]. This was precisely where the 24-
week and 48-week cycle lows developed and indicated strength. The labeled 
Wave ii in Fig 7-9 is actually the start of the rally and formed a high (towards the 
top of the weekly Price Equilibrium Cloud) to form the Wave [i] while the 
correction back into the cycle lows was the Wave [ii]. From there it has moved 
higher to form a 5-wave rally to see a high in the weekly Wave a of Wave iii.

Therefore, as you can see, the combination of bullish cycles, and once the 
hourly bearish divergence broke, the positive 4-hour and hourly HEW RSI and

While following the bullish momentum and Price Equilibrium Clouds, 
referring back to Fig 7-8, the wave count required some retrospective research. 
The problem seen was that there were several brief corrections that made it 
difficult to really be certain of the count. There are two ways to deal with this 
type of development. 
It is likely, with such scant corrections and multiple shallow Wave v's is that as 
the analysis progresses we would be looking for Wave v highs. This would imply 
that perhaps at the final high the count would have been a Wave -iii- high and 
thus we'd be looking for a Wave -iv- correction followed by the Wave -v-.

Because we know the limits of the Wave -iv- due to alternation the limits of the 
Wave -iv- could be used as a stop and reverse. 
Additionally, it would be possible to note the breakdown of the rally by 
observing the consolidation around the hourly Price Equilibrium Cloud initially 
and followed by price breaking below the 4-hour Price Equilibrium Cloud. It was 
not satisfactory, but the break level to exit any long positions should have been 
made following the break of the hourly corrective low and then the 4-hour Price 
Equilibrium Cloud low. Even then, it was just for a correction and as seen the 24-
week and 48-week cycles continues to push price higher towards the daily Wave 
a of Wave iii. 
This entire process demonstrates the benefit of utilizing a range of techniques 
that provide a stronger approach to analysis. In this way, while there can be 
confusion in the Harmonic Elliott Wave count (especially in the final waves) 
there are other supporting techniques to help us navigate the structure.

momentum divergence. This has already been discussed in Chapter Six. In Fig 7-
10, the Dow Jones Industrial Index saw a reversal lower in early December 2014.

CROSS-MARKET ANALYSIS 
I shall cover two complementary approaches of cross-market analysis here, the 
first in terms of cycles and the second in terms of expectations in wave structure. 
The objective is basically to ensure that the general timing expectations and/or 
directional implications are basically matching. 
CYCLE BASED OUTLOOKS 
Cross currency cyclic outlooks are basically a way of noting correlated 
directional development. This needn't necessarily imply that each currency pair 
must have the same Harmonic Elliott Wave structure, but more that the individual 
wave structures are uniformly bullish/bearish and with cycle lows (in particular) 
not too divergent from each other. This tends to confirm the general directional 
expectation. The more difficult element is working out how the group of currency 
pairs are likely to develop within their own Harmonic Elliott Wave structure. 
Let's take a look at four currency pairs that have consistent monthly cycles. 
Dec 1 984 
Feb 1 993 
Apr 2001 
Jun 2009 
Aug 2011 
Fig 7-12. Monthly bearish cycles in GBPUSD 
The chart in Fig 7-12 is the same as the chart in Fig 6-20. To recap, it suggests 
that we have a current bearish outlook that suggests a low around the 3 rd quarter 
in 2017. As discussed in Chapter 6, my expectation is that it will remain above 
the 1985 low at 1 .0370. That we are seeing a deep correction in Wave (ii) while

EURUSD has lost a greater percentage of its value from the end of the triangle, 
suggests that the cycles in GBPUSD are less aggressive. Nevertheless, the 
outlook does appear bearish. The Harmonic Elliott Wave structure that is implied 
is a 5-wave decline in Wave (C).

In the Australian Dollar seen in Fig 7-1 3, it appears that we have seen a 5-wave

201 6, it would appear to suggest a rally back above the Wave EFB and in 5-

then suggest a second 5-wave decline in the higher degree Wave (C). Considering 
the approximate 1 5-year cycle (second largest cycle) would expect a low in the 
early years of the 2030's. However, for the relatively near term (estimated) 
forecast for the next low is around the third quarter 2016. 
Another currency pair that has consistent fixed cycles is actually the Dollar 
Index itself, shown in Fig 7-1 4. Compared to GBPUSD and AUDUSD, the Dollar 
Index is quoted as a direct currency while the other two are quoted as indirect 
currencies (The Dollar Index is quoted as an amount of the other currency while 
an indirect currency is quoted as an amount of Dollars to the other currency.) 
Therefore, declines in GBPUSD and AUDUSD reflect Dollar strength while in 
the Dollar Index the declines represent Dollar lows. This is quite useful because 
we can get a sense of approximate timings of both Dollar highs and lows. 
Therefore, in the current rally in the Dollar Index, we can estimate that it will find 
a high between August 201 6  and August 201 7.

In summary, looking at the combined information of two directly quoted 
currency pairs and two indirectly quoted currency pairs, we have a rough 
guideline to where we can expect trends to reverse. By following the Harmonic 
Elliott Wave structure it should help in establishing the structures. From the 
current viewpoint, I get the feeling that Foreign Exchange currency movements 
are going to see stronger trending moves in quite strong swings rather similar to 
the 1970's and 1 980's. 
CROSS CURRENCY OUTLOOKS 
Quite often I have subscribers that advise me that they only trade one currency 
pair. In particular this occurs in the European currencies - EURUSD, USDCHF 
or GBPUSD. I do try and persuade them to consider looking at the analysis of 
generally correlated markets. Within the Europeans currencies in particular there 
is generally (but not necessarily permanent) correlation between all three of the 
currencies mentioned above. To a certain extent the Dollar Index can often 
provide information also, although there are obviously influences from USDJPY, 
AUDUSD along with the minor currency pairs. Indeed, there are often occasions 
where correlation is present but the individual wave counts can be different. 
While it's not always worthwhile dreaming up a scenario that would explain the 
outcome one particular currency, there are occasions where, say, EURUSD 
corrects in a Wave (ii) while USDCHF needs to correct in a Wave (b) of Wave 
(iii). Clearly, this really useful information because we can never know in 


=== PAGE 172 ===

INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTI WAVE WITH INDICATORS ! 167 
advance how deep a Wave (ii) will retrace but we can get a better idea of a Wave 
(b) or Wave (iii) - even if that could generate a deep Wave (b). Thus, we can 
begin to match the corrections within the respective corrective structures and 
unless EURUSD generates stronger momentum than USDCHF, the odds will 
favor a more conservative pullback in Wave (ii). 
Let's look at one example:

Fig 7-16. Correlating unmatched wave counts in EURUSD and USDCHF 
In Fig 7-16 there are two charts side by side. On the left is the daily chart of 
EURUSD as it declined from the Wave (i) and Wave (ii) that began from 1 .3993. 
To the right is the daily chart ofUSDCHF from the peak at the top left of the 
chart when the Swiss National Bank abandoned the minimum floor rate to the 
Euro, and decreased the interest rate to negative -0.75o/o. Clearly, the normal 
correlation between the two currency pairs was suspended for some months. 
Here we have two very different structures in place. EURUSD had corrected 
higher from the Wave (a) I (iii) low in a structure that was extremely challenging, 
so much so that in July I had thought price would resume the decline in Wave (c) 
I (iii). However, the market had other thoughts on its mind and since we had seen 
a double zigzag, there was also risk of a triple three. Even then, as the final rally 
developed in August 201 5, it rushed higher and peaked. 
By this point, USDCHF had formed a Wave -a- of Wave -iii- ofWave (c). I 
suspect this should form a Wave (i). So having seen the Wave -a- there was a 
question of how deep the Wave -b- I -iii- would retrace. The most challenging 
element of this was that there was no way in advance of knowing where the daily 
Wave (b) retracement would end in EURUSD. Therefore, it was necessary to 


=== PAGE 173 ===

168 I INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH IN DICATORS 
observe the lower degree wave count, of course in both but with a limiting factor 
in USDCHF. As this declined I felt the 66.7% retracement may hold - then the 
76.4%. However, as the decline continued and the Wave iii projections remaining 
strong, I had to resort to the 85.4% retracement. 
Fig 7-1 7. The triple three decline in USDCHF 
The lower degree development is difficult to provide in the hourly chart and it 
was only in the final Wave c that I was able to notate the chart. However, in the 
end the correction ended at the 85.7% retracement while EURUSD found its 
Wave (b) high at the 5 1 %  retracement. 
From the daily charts in Fig 7-1 6  these final legs in the respective corrections 
formed a spike top in EURUSD and a spike bottom in USDCHF. This could have 
specifically been noted in EURUSD because of the very long final Wave c in the 
triple three. Here we should apply the Elliott guideline that following the end of a 
3-wave move, price has a strong chance of reverting back to the span of the prior 
Wave b. 
If price had moved much below the 85.4% retracement in Wave -b- I -iii- in 
USDCHF, it would have signaled a failure and the likelihood that further Dollar 
losses would be seen. Therefore, it would act as a stop loss level and an entry into 
a bearish position. 
The example in Fig 7-1 7  is just one way in which correlated markets can 
provide information on what to expect because of their slightly different 
structures. One other alternative I have seen on a few occasions is the potential 
for one market to expect a final Wave v in a Wave -c- I -i- but which would have 
to match with and expanded flat in another market. While the first market will 


=== PAGE 174 ===

INTEGRATING HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE WITH INDICATORS I 169 
correct in Wave -ii-, the other market will retrace back to the Wave EFA and thus 
provide a guide to where the Wave -ii- will stall. 
There can be other methods of identifying stalling points including key moving 
averages (although they tend to be approximate) or key support and resistance 
and even correctly drawn trend lines. These can be linked to indicators that can 
provide information on whether momentum in slowing and risking a reversal, 
breaks of indicator bands such as Bollinger Bands and my Price Equilibrium 
Clouds. In this manner, the process of adding complementary analysis is vital to 
correlate price development - through Harmonic Elliott Wave - and other factors 
that build a more complete picture of price.

MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 
MARKET UPDATES 
DOW JONES INDUSTRIAL INDEX 
When I wrote Harmonic Elliott Wave back in 2010 I provided a few examples 
of forecasts. Of all, the Dow Jones Industrial Index has been the most astounding 
for me. I have been an Elliottician since 1 990. I worked in the Forex area of 
Barclays Bank in Hong Kong and began, like any other fledgling analyst, making 
lost of mistakes. In my first seminar that I attended I was assured that once the 
20-period moving average crossed the 60-period moving average it triggered a 
reversal. After making those call confidently I got every one of them wrong, 
despite the confident advice of the "guru" who taught us. So moving averages 
were quickly dismissed and I still do not use them to this day. 


=== PAGE 176 ===

MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 1 171 
I struggled with price patterns because they developed infrequently and there 
was so much price development where I felt "naked" without any real 
information. I used to follow a few other analysts on the major data vendors' 
networks - predominantly Reuters and Telerate - at that time and found that it 
was the Elliotticians that tended to provide the most accurate forecasts. So I went 
out and bought a book on Elliott Wave and followed these analysts and tried to 
work out why they made their call, and later tried to work out why it was right or 
why it had gone wrong. It took me around 1 8  months before I felt relatively 
confident of using the technique. Even then, there were always puzzles and 
confusion. However, in Forex I became one of the highest read analysts while I 
was in Hong Kong. 
That said, when I moved to Tokyo as a technical analysis specialist for 
Telerate, I found equity markets simply impossible to forecast. Therefore, where I 
could, I would avoid making calls on equities. That reluctance and inability to 
make any sense of equity markets remained with me until I stumbled upon the 
ratio structure that blessed the 3-wave structure. 
Therefore, in the first half of 201 0  while I was working on writing Harmonic 
Elliott Wave, I felt I had to provide a forecast on the DOW. This was the first 
analysis and forecast I made on equity markets using Harmonic Elliott Wave: 
Here it can be seen that there are three prospective target areas, at 
12,322-35 being the 1 76.4% projection n Wave (iii) and 85.4% projection 
in Wave (c); at 12,639-67 being the 190.2% projection in Wave (iii) and 
95.4% projection in Wave (c); andfinally at 12, 784-92 being the 195. 7% 
projection in Wave (iii) and wave equality target in Wave (c). Therefore, as 
a general target area this wave structure implies a Wave (iii) stalling 
between 12,322 and 12, 792.

In March 201 5  the rally to 1 8,288.63 provided the Wave (a) of Wave (v) and 
was followed by the Wave (b). The Wave (c) I (v) to complete the first 5-wave 
rally from inception, when it came, frustratingly deceived me and serves as a 
warning of what can occur. The high came on the 20th May 201 5  at 1 8,35 1 .36 -
so just 222 points below the 85.4% projection in the Wave ((V)). This was 1 .2o/o 
below high of the range that I suggested in May 2013 (in Fig 8.3a) but the chance 
of that target being seen was very, very unlikely. 
Fig 8-4a. The completion of the first 5-wave rally in the DOW from inception 


=== PAGE 181 ===

176 I MARKET U PDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 
I was ready to establish the final high, eagerly waiting for the culmination of 
the forecast I first made in 201 0. When it came, there was a complication that 
threw me off course. The Wave (c) is shown in Fig 8-4a although it is difficult to 
see clearly. Therefore, I have added another chart to show the hourly 
development in Fig 8-4b. 
Fig 8-4b. The hourly rally in Wave (c) I (v) to the final high in the DOW 
From the Wave (b) the development in Wave -i-, Wave -ii- and Wave -a- all 
went quite smoothly. Following the sharp drop from Wave a- it looked as if we 
should then rally to Wave -c- I -iii- but instead a mild expanding triangle 
developed, and in quite a complicated, cumbersome manner that provided a quite 
a challenge. Once the triangle was complete in Wave -b- the rally in Wave -c- I ­
iii- began. I followed this up to the Wave iii and Wave iv of Wave -c- I -iii- and 
then looked for a Wave v target that would match the Wave -iii-. This provided a 
36.8% projection in Wave v in order to reach the 1 76.4% projection in Wave -iii­
at 1 8,342.42 but was a little stronger at the 1 8,35 1 .36 high - a 1 78.5% projection. 
We now know this was the FINAL high but when this occurred and saw the 
deeper reversal that would have broken below the Wave -iv- support area I began 
to consider a larger expanding triangle in Wave -b-. 
It took some months of indifferent development, mostly sideways, before the 
rising weekly/daily channel support, shown in Fig 8-4a, together with an 
impossible set of conflicts between the DOW, S&P and NASDAQ, to force me to 
look back at the high. What actually occurred is that the Wave v of Wave -iii­
only reached the 1 72.5o/o projection but required a minor correction and followed 
by a Wave -v- that was only 1 2%. I have included this in the description just to 
highlight the way that even minor anomalies can cause quite a few problems at 
times. 


=== PAGE 182 ===

MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 1 177 
Now that we know that the high is in place, there is already another forecast 
that can be made right now - that once the high has been seen we should see the 
normal retracements into the span of the prior Wave (b) of Wave (v) - and 
probably to the rising channel low. Beyond that, we also have the higher degree 
Wave ((B)) of Wave ((V)) that would imply that an approximate 30% decline to 
the 12,035 - 13,338. However, I would also give allowance, because the Wave (( 
B )) was so brief, for the potential to approach the Wave (( IV )) more closely. 
The unknown issue here is the timeframe. Note the Wave ((B)) I ((V)) at the top 
right corner of the spreadsheet between the Wave (( A )) and Wave (( B )) in Fig 
8.4.

Fig 8-5. The decline from the 1990 high in the Nikkei Index to the low in 2008 
Because the recovery from the Wave (c) in April 2003 developed in 3-waves I 
was forced to offer two options: either we would see a 5-wave decline in the third 
and final Wave (a) of a triple three or we may have been seeing an expanded flat. 
From Fig 8-5 it can be seen that the final decline to the 6,994.90 low was very 
sharp and certainly shows no sign of a possible Wave iv in the final stages. This 


=== PAGE 183 ===

178 I MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 
tends to suggest a preference for an expanded flat. To confirm this I have 
provided the structure of the decline in Fig 8-6. 
Fig 8-6. A double zigzag lower in the Nikkei Index to form Wave EFB 
By observing where there could be alternation, if this had been a 5-wave 
decline, it is quite clear that the structure does not adhere to an impulsive 
sequence but does work well in a double zigzag. Therefore, this can be 
considered an expanded flat Wave EFB and should therefore imply a 5-wave 
recovery back to just above the peak at Wave EFA at 1 8,300-39, but will be 
Fig 8-7. The weekly rally in the Wave EFC rally to complete Wave (X)

preferable to hold below the prior Wave (b) at 20,833.2 1  in April 2000. Having

Wave (x) at 22,757.00. 
Compared with the earlier and relatively straightforward rally in Wave (a) of 
Wave (iii), seen in Fig 8-7, the Wave (c) was far more complicated with both a

-c- of Wave -iii-.

Fig 8-7a. The latter stages of the Wave EFC rally towards the Wave (v) 
Note the alternation across the Wave (ii) & Wave (iv), Wave -ii- and Wave -iv­
and Wave ii and Wave iv. There appears to be a general tendency for Wave v 
targets, as the eventual high is approached, to be a relatively short projection 
ratio. Indeed, the Wave (v) was a mere 1 5.8%. 
Combining the Harmonic Elliott Wave count with the rising wedges (and note 
these are patterns rather than individual waves) the rally to the final high 
developed in a rising wedge but began from the Wave b of Wave iii and through 
the Wave -iv- low and also Wave (b) of Wave (v) low. The break of the wedge 
support triggered losses to the Wave (b) of Wave ( v) as would be expected - then 
dipping below and followed by a retracement to the broken wedge low. 
Note: the catalyst for the decline from the high was the sharp losses in the 
Chinese stock market. 
The bearish divergences in HEW RSI tended to coincide well with the Wave -
a- I -iii- peak, and later the Wave -iii- of Wave (c) peak and also the Wave (c) I 
(iii) peak. While these provoked corrections they always saw the respective

Projections 20269.52 : 2:0388.69 : 20521.11 : 20582 .• 02: 20656.17 : 20703.84 : 20775.35 : 20884.52 : 21087.85 : 2:1400.36 :  21465.24 21593.69 21712.86 
Fig 8-8. The spreadsheet showing the Wave (c) I (iii) and Wave EFC targets 
The spreadsheet in Fig 8-8 highlights the range of targets in both lower degree 
and higher degree waves. Specifically, the Wave (iii) targets are suggesting two 
targets at likely precise levels, at the 238.2% projection at 1 8,648.20 or as high as 
the 261 .8o/o at 19,689.73. In fact, it is possible to see a Wave (iii) just above the 
261 .8% projection because this will be in the general 261 .8%-298.7% projection 
range. However, due to the limited room left for the Wave (v) to preferable 
remain below 20,833.21 but not above 2 1 ,000, the Wave (v) projection was a 
more difficult decision but most likely to stall between the 33.3% - 41 .4% 
projection although there was no rule to suggest it couldn't be higher. Therefore, 
keen observation in the lower degree fractals would have been required. The final 
high came at 20,952.71 .  
The two forecasts above are examples of how Harmonic Elliott Wave can 
navigate the development of a structure over a period of many years. Of course, it 
is possible that a forecast for a market could break down. In the example of the 
DOW above, while the final high was missed due to a rare anomaly, the 
subsequent development will always finally break down due to a structure that is 
inconsistent with another push to an expected Wave (v). At other times, the same 
potential for a breakdown in the lower degree structure can also signal a break in 
the impulsive development.

CURRENT MARKET FORECASTS 
In this section of the chapter I shall be providing examples of forecasts I have 
made over the past few years using Harmonic Elliott Wave, some of which have 
not yet been fulfilled and some that have been completed. I will also include not 
only long-term forecasts but also short-term to demonstrate that the ratio 
methodology is consistent across all levels of the wave degree. 
GBPUSD 
Following the Wave (A) that developed from the 2. 1 160 high in November 
2007, I had obviously been expecting a correction in Wave (B). As would be 
expected, the correction was a very complicated, erratic correction GBPUSD 
made its high two months after EURUSD during July 201 4. I had been waiting 
for this development for several years. The chart below in Fig 8-9 which was 
shown earlier in Chapter 6.
