Fig 8-9. A multi-decade development of cycles implying losses in GBPUSD 
I have been following these cycles for many years and knew that we should see 
losses, but it wasn't until Harmonic Elliott Wave provided the structure that I 
could clarify the entire picture. Before Harmonic Elliott Wave, and using 
traditional Elliott Wave, I had been counting the rally to the 1 .9050 high, labeled 
Wave -iii- (in April 1 988), as the Wave -v- and therefore the Wave (a) high. From 


=== PAGE 187 ===

182 I MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 
there I considered the decline to the Wave (b) as an expanded flat. Harmonic 
Elliott Wave made the structure far more logical. 
Thus, by using Harmonic Elliott Wave I now count the rally to the Wave (a) at 
2.0035 in September 1992, the correction to the Wave (b) low at the combination 
of three cycle lows in June 2001 that provided confirmation of a reversal higher. 
Finally, the Wave (c) ended at 2.1 1 60 from where we saw the 5-wave decline to 
Wave (A) around the intermediate cycle lows in June 2009. In July 2014 I finally 
saw the Wave (B) high at 1 .7 1 90. This registered a bearish divergence in the daily 
chart. 
The cycles are very clearly bearish through to (around) August 201 7  and 
should be relatively direct. Where will these losses target? It is actually quite an 
interesting question. Following the 3-wave recovery from the 1 .0370 low, an 
ambiguity arose. What were we seeing? The first leg of a complex correction? 
Perhaps a flat or expanded flat - or even triangle? The problem was that even a 
chart back to the early 1 970's did not provide sufficient information to clarify 
what exactly where we were in the larger picture. I then found a site where I 
could get much longer data. 
Fig 8-9a. The chart of GBPUSD from the 1930's 
It is difficult even now to be absolutely certain, but as far as I can see from this 
chart that the Dollar topped out against the British Pound following a 5-wave 
decline in (I assume) a Wave (a). From there the recovery developed, as 
suggested above, in 3-waves that stalled within the span of the bearish Wave b of 
Wave v - the normal first reversal following a Wave v. This tends to suggest that 
the 2. 1 160 high was the Wave -c- and therefore we are due a correction in Wave ­
x-. Equally, this implies that we need see a 5-wave decline in Wave c that should 


=== PAGE 188 ===

MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS 1 183 
complete Wave -x- and above the 1 .0370 low. From there it implies a rally back 
above 2. 1 1 60 in 3-waves. 
MUMBAI SENSEX 
Fig 8-1 0. The Mumbai Sensex moving towards the final rally to the Wave (C) 
As I began writing this book around the end of 2014, I was requested by a 
friend to provide a forecast for the Mumbai Sensex. I was advised that the general 
forecasts by (I believe) Elliotticians were for an approach to 1 00,000. I had 
written the start of the analysis by early 201 5  but was then distracted by other 
work. However, the initial estimated target I had was around 32,800. 
So, as I am completing the final editing and formatting of the book, we have 
already seen the final high at 30,024.74. This is shown in Fig 8-1 0. 
Two to three years ago I had been looking at this market and has already 
decided that the 2 1 ,206.77 high in January 2008 was the top of the first 5 -wave 
rally and therefore implied a Wave (A). The sharp decline there was a chance that 
a complex correction may occur since it appeared to develop in a 3-wave decline 
to form Wave (B) at 7,697.39. While this could generate a complex correction, 
the rally has been more consistent with an impulsive rally to complete Wave (C) 
that I feel is likely to be Wave (I). The chart for the Wave (C) is shown in Fig 8-
10a.

straightforward but like the rather cramped space in the Wave (v), the high came 
in rather earlier than expected and thus not reaching my original estimate. 
Therefore, the fact that I had only daily bars to observe made the process a lot 
less precise.

In Fig 8-1 1 the spreadsheet displays the ratio development in Wave (c) of 
Wave (iii) of the larger Wave (C), the latter plotted in the lower section of the 
spreadsheet. In normal circumstances I would be observing the lower degree 
development to confirm a valid set of ratios during their development. The Wave 
-iii- was seen at the 294.8% projection that was accompanied by close to a 
176.4% projection in the Wave -c-. Thus, following the Wave -ii- that was 8 1 .9%, 
the Wave -iv- would have to be shallow - in this case just below 9% - we then had 
to match a Wave -v- with the higher degree Wave (c) I (iii). This turned out to be 
at the 594.4% projection at 27,354.99. 
Following the Wave (iii), the Wave (iv) was once again very, very shallow and 
the Wave (v) a mere 2 1  % ... 
There is one more approach could have been employed and that's in the 
potential for correlation with other major markets. Having discussed the targets in 
both the Dow Jones Industrial Index and also in the Nikkei Index, there is likely 
to be a rough correlation in timing. Indeed, this was approximate with the DOW 
finding its high on the 20th May, the Mumbai Index on the 4th March and Nikkei 
on the 24th June. While not necessarily so close, the fact that all three were due 
major highs is encouraging for the overall perception that global stock markets 
are likely to tum lower. That recently the Chinese stock markets have suffered 
serious losses is another supporting factor. I have also considered the FTSE to 
have found its high also. 
PAST FORECASTS 
In this section I'd like to provide a few forecasts I have made over the past 4 
years. This is not only to provide evidence of the potential accuracy of Harmonic 
Elliott Wave but also, in a selection of forecasts, how the understanding of cycles 
and natural stalling points to provide a guide to how to approach forecasting. Of 
course, not all the forecasts I have made in the past have been fulfilled, but the 
process of noting where they have broken down is as vital to understand where a 
forecast has failed. 
EURUSD 
When the period of consolidation began from the November 2008 low at 
1 .2329, there was no real way of knowing how the correction would develop. 
Perhaps it could be a simple zigzag move but in reality there were a wide range of 
options from the zigzag, double zigzag, triple three, flat, expanded flat or triangle. 
This is always the challenge from the outset. In this particular case it was 
complicated by the fact that we had an irregular triangle. The expansion below 


=== PAGE 191 ===

the 1 .2329 low could have been just an expanded flat Wave efb. To cut a long 
story short, I realized that we were seeing an irregular triangle once we were 
moving lower in the Wave AD. It took a second attempt at calling the Wave AD 
target before I made the following forecast: 
I raised last week about the lack of room on the upside was 
and of course we know this broke higher. At this point, as I 
last week I feel this imp lies we had a very deep Wave "D. Thus, 
should now be in Wave "E that should come in 3-weves_

Wave AE 
Following the end of the Wave AD, on the 1 1th September 2012, I provided a 
forecast for the Wave AE of the triangle to stall between the 1 .3550-1 .3850 area. 
The forecast in Fig 8-12 read: 
"The concern I raised last week about the lack of room on the upside was 
timely ... and of course we know this broke higher. At this point, as I indicated last 
week, !feel this implies we had a very deep Wave AD. Thus, we should now be in 
Wave A£ that should come in 3-waves. 
The first target for the Wave (a) should be in the span of the prior Wave (b) 
between 1.3075 - 1.3275 and I suspect the higher. From there a correction in 
Wave (b) should develop before Wave (c) can reach the 50%-58.6% projection in 
Wave A£ between 1.3550-1.3850." 
In the end the triangle the Wave AE ended a little higher and just above the 
6 1 .8% projection at 1 .3993. This high was confirmed by, not only a Key Bearish 
Reversal Week as seen in Fig 8-13, but also a Key Bearish Reversal Month. 
Indeed, there were supporting bearish divergences running from the monthly 
chart down through the weekly and daily charts.

GOLD

had seen an initial decline down towards the prior Wave -b- of Wave -v- and 
towards the Wave -iv- low. From there some consolidation/corrective activity 
developed followed by a correction higher. It was at this point as I suspected we 
were seeing a Wave (b) correction that I provided a forecast in the November 27th 
2012 in the Harmonic Elliott Wave weekly report:

In the presentation I provided a chart displaying the cycles that are shown in 
Fig 8-13. These cycles are slightly different to the description of how cycles 
develop. The general expectation of cycles developing tidily into 2-4-8-1 6  etc., 
does not appear to be the case in Gold. Instead each larger cycle splits into three, 
within which, it reverts to dividing into two. I have also noted this in other 
markets, specifically USDCHF. I have used this methodology on a few markets 
and it certainly appears likely that, with the strong right translation, that we are 
due a cycle low over the first half of201 5, more likely the 2nd quarter. 
Fig 8-1 5a. The development of Gold from the 1 920.80 high 
How deep can this correction deepen? In the November 2012 forecast I 
suggested the 50%-58.6% retracement but this has already been tested. Therefore, 
it seems likely that we need to allow for the 66.7% retracement at 1 ,022.30. As 
things stand as they are now, price has been declining in a wedge and with price 
testing the wedge low. There is a very long and sturdy weekly bullish divergence 
and I suspect we shall not see a triple three. Therefore, I suspect we shall see a 
low by the time this book is published. 
This will form Wave (b) of Wave (iii) that suggests a minimum target at the 
361 .8%-398.4% projection between 2,271 .70 and 2,447.99, but perhaps we 
should keep in mind the 461 .8% projection at 2,749.45. 
On first reversal we should see a rally into the span of the prior Wave b-, 
perhaps initially maintaining below the wedge high but once this is broken the 
outlook should tum higher.

Fig 8-1 6. The forecast for the Wave [iii] and Wave [iv] in EURUSD 
On the 20th November 2013 I wrote in the forecast: 
"We had an expanded flat Wave [b) and shallow at 23.6% and should now see 
Wave [cl I [iiil reach the 238.2% projection at 1.3684 being just above the 
85.4% projection in Wave [c). Also note the 261.8% projection at 1.3720 though 
I'm less in favor of it being that high. This should generate a relatively shallow 
Wave [ivl (Wave fiil was 85.4% so look (or 14. 6% - 23. 6% before Wave [v} ends 
up around the 1.3832 high to form Wave -a- and then a correction which I feel 
could be quite deep. " 
The Wave [b] that I felt may have been completed on the 20th, actually dipped 
a little lower to the level, just below 1 .3400, on the 21 st November. However, 
from there the rally resumed. It was certainly not an easy process to follow 
because of the extremely erratic nature of the development. All the Elliott Wave 
analysts I saw during that time were bearish and in many ways, I can sympathize 
with them because it did look like a classic corrective wave. However, I was 
confident that we had just seen the 50% correction in daily Wave -iv- and thus 
was confident that this move was impulsive. In Fig 8-17, I have provided the 
follow-through to the Wave [iii] and Wave [iv], adding the wave relationships for 
two of the wave degrees. 


=== PAGE 196 ===

MARKET UPDATES AND EXAMPLE FORECASTS / 191 
Fig 8-17. The rally in EURUSD to the Wave [iii] and Wave [iv] in EURUSD 
The rally continued to follow solid harmonic wave ratios. Note the alternation 
in each of the Wave (iv) and Wave [iv] positions and how the relevant Wave v's 
and Wave (v)'s all match with their respective Wave v and Wave (v) target where 
I have been able to label. This demonstrates the truly fractal nature of price 
development. 
Finally, the Wave [iii] and the Wave [iv] occurred on a Non Farm Payroll 
release day. I was sitting there with a group on an Elliott Wave forum, the trading 
up to the release extremely listless, but it was actually the Non Farm Payrolls 
release that provided both the Wave [iii] high and the 14.6o/o retracement in Wave 
[iv] to then continue higher. 
AUDUSD 
In the example above in EURUSD, I have used the hourly chart to demonstrate 
the harmonic structure as compared to the earlier daily and weekly forecasts. As a 
final example, I shall add an example in the lower degree chart, in this case, using 
the base 5-minute chart that I habitually use for my daily analysis. I must add that 
this lower degree development is subject to more approximate projections 
because of the risk of mildly stronger/weaker Wave iii projections and occasional 
shortfalls in alternation. It is in these lower degrees, because of the often, frenetic 
development that can occur that there is little decision time when the targets 
deviate from the "perfect." This is where losses can be made if not paying 
attention.

APPENDIX I 195 
lj 
USING THE HARMONIC ELLIOTT WAVE SPREADSHEET 
One of the most common inquiries I receive is concerning the use of the 
spreadsheet. It looks daunting when first seen but is actually an extremely useful 
- and vital - tool. When I wrote Harmonic Elliott Wave I was still using a 
calculator to check ratios. By the time I completed the book I realized that 
because the lower wave degree targets must match the higher wave degree 
targets, that placing grids of ratios above/below each other that I could identify 
the areas where a (say) Wave -v- of Wave (c) of Wave (iii) will provide an area to 
observe. Indeed, this approach can be useful over three or four wave degrees. 
In addition to being able to estimate where the common targets will be, it is 
also useful for estimating where the Wave -iii- of Wave (c) of Wave (iii) can stall 
in order that the Wave -iv- (which we can estimate using alternation) and 
common Wave -v- targets will match the Wave (iii) target. There is still a range 
of alternatives because we don't know in advance whether the Wave -v- will be 


=== PAGE 201 ===

196 I APPENDIX 
as shallow as 38.2% or as deep as 76.4%. However, as the waves develop it will 
be easier to home in to the eventual combination. 
I have constructed the spreadsheet to allow the ratios to be adjusted. I do get 
quite a few people inquiring why I don't put every ratio on the grid. The answer 
is that there are so many that if they were all inserted then using the spreadsheet 
would be cumbersome, particularly for the larger projection ratios (361 .8%, 
398.4%, 423.6% etc.) that would extend to the right too far. Therefore, being able 
to adjust the ratios within the main viewing area is far more practical. The only 
cells that shouldn't be changed are the Wave (c) I (iii) and Wave (c) I (v) because 
they are linked from the Wave (iii) and Wave (v) inputs. 
In this section, I shall try to demonstrate how to use each part of the 
spreadsheet, how to generate estimates for waves and the methodology of homing 
in on the eventual targets.

Fig A-2. The spreadsheet for the values in Fig A-1 
In the lower section of the spreadsheet we can begin to insert the values of the 
relevant highs and lows. Wave -i- was at 0.9328 and Wave -ii- at 0.9307 being a 
50% retracement. From that point we then need to follow the development in 
Wave -a- in Fig Al. These levels are inserted into the higher section of the 
spreadsheet. The first entry will be the Wave -ii- that has been placed in the lower 
section. So Wave i was at 0.93 1 7, Wave ii at 0.93 12 being a 50o/o retracement. 
Then we would progress to the Wave a which was at 0.9341 and Wave b at 
0.9323. At this point we'd be able to begin estimating where the Wave iii may 
target. If we were able to identify the lower degree waves in Wave c I iii then we 
could use the next higher level of the spreadsheet to follow the development. 
Let's say this has been done and the Wave iii ended at 0.9354 - so that is placed 
in the Wave (iii) box. While the ration is 420%, if you look at the 423.6% 
projection to the far right, it can be seen that the price is at 0.9354. (This can 
happen in the 5-minute charts and below.) 
Having established the Wave iii it is then possible to estimate the Wave iv 
using alternation. The ratio was 38. 1% and the sum of Wave ii and Wave iv came 
to 88. 1 %. Finally, we then need to establish the Wave v - at 0.9377 being an 83% 
projection. This high will therefore form the Wave -a- of Wave -iii- and the price 
placed in the Wave (a) box in the lower spreadsheet. 
The next step is to then observe the next lower degree rally in Wave -c-. 


=== PAGE 203 ===

198 I APPENDIX 
1111.23% 
225.06%

Fig A-3. The next rally in Wave -c- of Wave -iii- in USDCHF 
Fig A-3 shows the spreadsheet once again. Having determined that the Wave v 
was at 0.9377, this is inserted into the Wave (a) cell in the lower section of the 
spreadsheet. From that point it would be necessary to observe the lower degree 
development in Wave -b- to identify the low. This occurred at 0. 93 14 and is 
therefore inserted to the right of the Wave (a) cell. At this point we could observe 
for the range of Wave -iii- targets that could be possible. There were quite a few 
options but, of course, it had to be a reasonable margin above the Wave -a- at 
0.9377. 
So, with a range of potential targets I mind, the same procedure must be 
followed as before in the Wave -a-. Note the levels in the lower degree 
development in Wave -c- that would be inserted into the higher section. By the 
time the Wave iii at 0.9429 being very close to a 238.2% projection, it would be 
possible to estimate the Wave iv and then look for the Wave v projections that 
would match a Wave -iii- target. Observe momentum and the lower degree 
development. Eventually the Wave -v- will be established (at 0.9504) and will 
form the Wave (c) ofWave (i). 
The next stage will be to insert the 0.9257 starting price of this move into the 
lower section and then place the 0.9504 price (being the Wave (i)) and then 
observe the development in Wave (ii). Once established the same process of 
measuring the lower degree waves in Wave (a) as described above. 
I go through this procedure every day and actually have two or three 
worksheets for each market. This will allow an entire range of wave degrees to be

under construction - from the 5-minute chart through to the monthly chart in 
order to make estimates for the higher degree Wave (iii) targets.

Fig A-4. The DOW starting from the approach to Wave (( I )) to current day 
Fig A-4 displays the rally towards the end of the first Wave (( I )) from the 
inception of the DOW through to the current moment. The chart shown has been 
shown on a semi-log scale that distorts the actual structure but actually shows a 
clear harmonic structure. However, all measurements are made on a linear basis 
which I find most accurate. As a guide, the Wave (( IV )) at 6,469.95 in 2009 was 
actually a 54% retracement. As can be seen, at the time of writing, we are seeing 
the final Wave (( C )) of Wave (( V )) developing now, and not too far away.

chart from the Wave (( IV )) low.

Fig A-6. Three levels of the spreadsheet from the inception of the DOW 
Of course, I cannot be totally certain of the monthly development because I 
would have had to live through several hundred years of observing the lower 
degree waves. However, what I have noted is that the rally from the (estimated) 
25 starting price has provided some very interesting ratios. The Wave (( II )) that 
occurred in the early 1930's crash was a deep 95.7%. There's nothing unusual 
about that. It is from here that the sheer size of the Wave (( III )) projection of 
3,920% is one that is difficult to consider when the DOW began. However, I note 
that in terms of the manner in which the ratios below 1 00% are added to 1 OOo/o, 
200% etc., seems to suggest that just as a 392% projection is valid, then adding 
an additional zero fits that methodology. There is just no other market I have data 
for that began so long ago. I also attempted to ensure that the Wave (( A )) and 


=== PAGE 207 ===

202 I APPENDIX 
Wave (( C )) ofWave (( III )) also had valid impulsive ratios. Even the Wave (( C 
)) projection was a 1 0  times multiple of 1 4 1 .4% - at 1 ,418.8%. That the Wave (( 
IV )) retraced 55% is quite in line with the very deep Wave (( II )). 
So in general the pattern of development, when considering ratios, is very 
consistent with the projection cluster ratios. If we take an 85.4% projection in 
Wave (( V )) the target is at 1 8,573.78 and I suspect we'll see the rally from 
inception stall in around this area. 
ALTERNATION THROUGH THE FRACTALS 
In the description above, in which I have described the methodology of using 
the spreadsheet to identify projection targets through wave relationships, the 
fractal nature of price development can already be observed within the two charts 
provided. The next stage is to demonstrate that the ratio structure is valid across 
all wave degrees (fractals) and that they all develop in a manner that, to me, holds 
amazing beauty with the vast majority of waves displaying alternation. I shall 
once again use the Dow Jones Industrial Index, mainly because it has one of the 
longest historical data sets. I will begin again by repeating the chart from 
inception as seen in Fig A - 1 ,  but will fill in the lower degree fractals with ratios. 
Following this I shall work through selected waves from the Wave (( IV )) 
onwards to the top of Wave (( A )). 
The chart in Fig A-7 is once again displayed in semi-log scale, but price ratios 
are measured in a linear scale. You may wish to refer back to Chapter 3 in which 
I described the ratio methodology, noting the key cluster areas for Wave (iii)'s 
and noting that Alternation invariably sees a sum of Wave (ii) and Wave (iv) of 
around 1 00%. As a basic reminder, the template for Wave (iii)'s is: 
1 76.4% - 1 98.7o/o 
223.6% 
238.2% 
261 .8o/o - 298.7% 
then: 
X23.6% 
X38.2% 
X61 .8% - X98.7% (ie. X will increase at each step by 1)

As compared to Fig A-4, I have added the next lower degree waves in a lighter 
gray color. For each wave degree I have also labeled the ratio for each particular 
wave. There are some instances where alternation has not quite been satisfied. In 
the rally from Wave (( II )) the sum of Wave (ii) and Wave (iv) is only just above 
70%. However, the Wave (b) of Wave (iii) was exceptionally deep. In the Wave 
c) of Wave (iii) the sum of Wave -ii- and Wave -iv- is around 74% but there is no 
deeper Wave -b- of Wave -iii- or Wave -v-. Shortfalls do occasionally occur. In 
the Wave (( C )) of Wave (( III )) alternation is very good, although a mild short 
fall in the sum ofWave -ii- and Wave -iv- in Wave (c) ofWave (iii). 
Otherwise the ratios are mostly standard for each wave - just the Wave (iii) of 
Wave (( C )) I (( C )) being 2414.4% - so just above the 238.2% equivalent but 
note the 41 .4% which is a harmonic ratio. 
I find the fact that these ratios have been valid from when trading began is 
absolutely incredible. These are not ratios I have made up. These are a product of 
mass psychology that must be inherent in the human psyche. That they exist from 
this major wave degree right down to the weekly, daily, hourly and 5 -minute 
wave degrees is nothing more than astounding. To demonstrate this, I shall now 
provide a series of wave degrees, starting with the rally from the Wave (( IV )) 
low and then taking selected waves down to the lower wave degrees.

commencement of an impulsive rally (or decline) is so sharp that the Wave i and

swing lows are the only peaks/troughs that should be used. It also demonstrates 
that the zigzag lines that are generally drawn to depict Elliott Wave counts, are

While these lower degree waves can become quite complicated due to the 
numerous minor corrections and sharp moves, the ratios are once again perfect. 
I hope this series of charts, from the monthly through to the 5-minute charts, 
demonstrates how price develops in fractals that are linked harmoniously through 
a common ratio structure, seamlessly and with incredibly beauty. Without 
suggesting any criticism of R.N. Elliott's findings, these charts highlight how the 
3-wave structure in Harmonic Elliott Wave is vastly superior to the original 5-
wave structure.
